Title: Censura Orientalis Ecclesiae de praecipuis nostri seculi haereticorum dogmatibus, Hieremiae Constantinopolitano Patriarchae iudicii et mutuae communionis caussa ab orthodoxae doctrinae adversariis non ita pridem oblatis, ab eodem Patriarcha Constantinopolitano ad Germanos Graece conscripta (Patriarch Jeremias II's first Answer to the Tuebingen theologians on the Augsburg Confession, 1576, in Latin)
Creator: Jeremias II (Tranos), Patriarch of Constantinople; translated from the Greek into Latin, with annotations, by Stanislaus Socolovius (Stanislaw Sokolowski), theologian to King Stephen of Poland, and dedicated to Pope Gregory XIII
Publisher: In officina typographica Lazari (the Lazarus press), Cracow
Publication Date: 1582
Rights: Public Domain (printed 1582)
Language: lat
Source: Internet Archive item bub_gb_HBBAiEghEYQC, file bub_gb_HBBAiEghEYQC_djvu.txt (National Central Library of Rome copy, from the library of the Collegio Romano of the Society of Jesus; Google scan), fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for Orthodoxy under the Ottomans), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: licenceurl Creative Commons Public Domain Mark 1.0
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long s normalised to s (0 occurrences); where the printer's long s was read by the OCR as 'f' it is left as read. Rights basis restated: a book printed at Cracow in 1582 is public domain by date.
Content note: SECOND WITNESS: the roman-type body is readable Latin (long s often read as f, ligatures and 'ae' as 'q' or '^'), the italic dedication and preface are heavily garbled. This is the Catholic polemicist Socolovius's own Latin rendering of the Patriarch's first Answer (dated 15 May 1576), made independently of the Tuebingen theologians' Latin printed in the 1584 Acta et scripta (vendored separately); the two are the only Latin witnesses in the corpus, and the Greek is in neither. Socolovius's dedication to Gregory XIII and his annotations against the Greeks are his, not the Patriarch's. Order in the file: title page (about line 1-30, 'Cracoviae, in officina typographica Lazari, 1582' read as 'cIo Io XXCII'), dedication, the Answer under chapter headings ('Caput'), Socolovius's notes. Only the first Answer; the second (1579) and third (1581) are in the 1584 Acta.
